Criterion-level results

The collection contains 155 papers. List experiments are included. Classes 1–3 enter the score summaries. The assessment scope and classification decisions are recorded with each report.

Bars show criteria scored 1 / applicable experiment assessments. Each experiment contributes one observation per criterion; N/A is excluded from that criterion’s denominator.

Shared scoring prompt

Download Markdown
Act as an independent reviewer of experimental reporting. Evaluate whether a competent applied researcher can reconstruct each experiment and its statistical analysis using only the published main article.

Do not evaluate novelty, substantive importance, journal prestige, or whether you agree with the conclusions. Do not seek a predetermined winner. Apply exactly the same evidentiary standard to every paper.

## Papers

Review the following published main articles:

[Insert article citations, URLs, or attached PDFs here.]

## Source restriction

Use only the published main article, including its main-text tables, figures, table notes, figure notes, and footnotes.

Do not consult online appendices, supplementary information, preregistrations, registries, replication files, code, or external methodological references. A reference to excluded material does not supply the omitted information.

Use the 20 core criteria below to define the assessment. If an additional reporting checklist is supplied, use it only to define its scoring items and components; it is not evidence about the papers. If no additional checklist is supplied, omit the separate checklist assessment.

Do not search for or substitute any external reporting standard or checklist. Online access is permitted only to obtain the identified published main articles. All paper-specific evidence must come from those main articles.

## Unit of evaluation

First identify every distinct randomized experiment.

Classify each as:

1. Primary experiment
2. Supporting experiment with a formal statistical analysis
3. Brief diagnostic randomization or robustness check
4. Pilot with no reported outcome
5. Observational analysis

Score categories 1–3 separately. List categories 4–5 without scoring them as experiments.

Do not combine all randomizations into a single article-level score. Report primary, supporting, and diagnostic experiments separately.

A follow-up using the original randomized assignment belongs to the original experiment. A new randomized sample or manipulation is a separate experiment.

## Binary scoring

Use:

- **1 = fully meets the reporting standard**
- **0 = does not fully meet the reporting standard**
- **N/A = logically inapplicable**

Do not award partial credit. If a material component required by a criterion is missing, score the entire criterion 0.

Use N/A only when the concept cannot apply to the design. Failure to report information is 0, not N/A.

## Evidence rule

A regression equation is not required when a regression table and its notes jointly make the analysis completely unambiguous.

A regression table may earn full credit when it clearly identifies:

- Outcome
- Treatment or experimental contrast
- Treatment and control coding
- Omitted or reference category
- Analysis unit
- Estimator
- Covariates or fixed effects in each specification
- Standard-error procedure
- Clustering or other dependence adjustment
- Analysis sample size
- Effect estimate
- Uncertainty measure
- Correspondence between the table column and the result discussed in the text

Do not reduce a score merely because information appears in a table rather than prose.

Conversely, merely naming an estimator—such as OLS, AMCE, marginal means, conditional AMCE, or bootstrap—does not earn credit when the reader cannot reconstruct how it was implemented.

## Design map

Before scoring, create the following map for every scored experiment:

- Target population
- Eligibility criteria
- Recruitment procedure
- Field dates and setting
- Number recruited and analyzed
- Experimental arms or randomized attributes
- Unit of randomization
- Allocation probabilities and restrictions
- Exact treatment and comparator
- Outcome definitions and timing
- Estimand
- Estimator or statistical model
- Analysis unit
- Variable coding and reference categories
- Covariates and fixed effects
- Standard-error and inference procedure
- Handling of clustering or repeated observations
- Missing-data and attrition rules
- Effect estimates and uncertainty

Cite the page, table, figure, or footnote supporting every entry. Write “NR” when an element is not reported. Do not infer standard practice.

## Core reporting criteria

Score every experiment on the following criteria.

### Experimental design

1. **Population and recruitment:** Target population, eligibility, recruitment, and major exclusions are fully reported.
2. **Experimental conditions:** Treatment and comparator, or all randomized attributes and levels, are reported precisely enough to reproduce.
3. **Randomization:** Unit, probabilities or ratio, sequence-generation process, and restrictions are fully reported.
4. **Outcome measurement:** Outcomes, coding, timing, and primary or secondary status are fully reported.
5. **Participant flow:** Numbers recruited, randomized, exposed, followed, and analyzed are reported as applicable.

### Statistical methods

6. **Estimand:** The causal or descriptive quantity being estimated is explicitly defined.
7. **Estimator/model:** The statistical operation used to estimate the estimand is reconstructable from the text, table, or table notes.
8. **Analysis unit:** The reader can determine whether observations are participants, profiles, choices, sessions, or another unit.
9. **Coding and reference categories:** Treatment coding, outcome coding, omitted categories, and reference levels are explicit.
10. **Specification:** Covariates, fixed effects, weights, transformations, and column-to-column specification changes are explicit.
11. **Dependence and inference:** Standard errors, clustering, repeated-observation dependence, bootstrap or permutation procedures, and confidence-interval construction are explicit.
12. **Missingness and attrition:** Inclusion rules, missing outcomes, exclusions, attrition, and corresponding adjustments are explicit.
13. **Multiplicity and analysis status:** Multiple-testing procedures and prespecified versus exploratory analyses are distinguished.

### Results

14. **Analysis sample:** The number of observations used in each focal analysis is reported.
15. **Group quantities:** Relevant control means, treatment means, marginal means, or other raw quantities are reported.
16. **Effect and uncertainty:** Effect estimates and standard errors or confidence intervals are reported.
17. **Model-to-result traceability:** Each focal numerical result can be connected directly to a particular outcome, contrast, specification, sample, and inference procedure.
18. **Robustness analyses:** Alternative specifications and sensitivity analyses are explained sufficiently to understand what changed.

### Validity assessment

19. **Internal-validity threats:** Attrition, noncompliance, spillovers, demand effects, measurement problems, and other relevant threats are discussed.
20. **Inferential scope:** The paper distinguishes the randomized contrast from broader claims about mechanisms, populations, and settings.

## Component-level scoring

Treat each criterion as a compound item when it contains multiple required components.

Display the score and evidence for every component. Give the overall criterion a score of 1 only when every applicable component scores 1. Do not count component scores separately in the criterion totals.

For example:

- Criterion 1 components: target population, eligibility, recruitment, major exclusions
- Criterion 3 components: unit, allocation probability or ratio, sequence generation, restrictions
- Criterion 4 components: outcome definition, coding, timing, primary/secondary status
- Criterion 11 components: standard errors, clustering, repeated-observation handling, resampling procedures, confidence-interval construction
- Criterion 20 components: randomized contrast, mechanism scope, population scope, setting scope

Explain every component-level N/A judgment.

## Design-specific standards

### Ordinary treatment-control experiments

Award full statistical-model credit when the main-text regression table and notes jointly identify the outcome, treatment indicator, omitted control condition, analysis unit, adjustments, inference method, sample size, estimate, and uncertainty.

Do not require a redundant mathematical equation.

### List experiments

Require:

- Control list
- Treatment list
- Sensitive item
- Randomization mechanism
- Difference-in-means estimator
- Analysis unit
- Variance or confidence-interval procedure
- Sample size
- Numerical estimate

### Conjoint experiments

Award full credit only when the main article reports:

- Number of tasks and profiles per respondent
- Attribute levels and assignment probabilities
- Restrictions on profile construction
- Outcome coding
- Exact estimand
- Estimating model or computational procedure
- Analysis unit
- Reference categories
- Treatment of paired profiles and repeated observations
- Respondent-level clustering or another justified dependence adjustment
- Analysis sample size
- Direct mapping from the model to every focal AMCE, marginal mean, or related figure

The statement “we estimate AMCEs,” a citation to a standard conjoint article, or a pointer to an appendix is insufficient.

### Brief diagnostic randomizations

Score these separately. Do not average them with primary or formally analyzed experiments.

If only two percentages are reported without an explicit contrast, uncertainty calculation, or arm-specific sample sizes, score the missing statistical criteria 0.

## Additional checklist assessment

Complete this section only if an additional reporting checklist is supplied. Do not obtain one independently.

After completing the reconstructability assessment, provide a separate table for the supplied checklist.

For compound checklist items, divide the item into its required components. Give the item a score of 1 only if every applicable component is fully reported; otherwise score it 0.

Keep article-level items—such as title, funding, conflicts, registration, and data access—separate from experiment-level methods and results.

Do not use the overall checklist total as a measure of statistical-model clarity.

Report separately:

1. Overall completeness under the supplied checklist
2. Experimental-design reporting
3. Statistical-method transparency
4. Results transparency
5. Internal-validity discussion

## Required output

Produce:

1. An inventory of experiments and their classifications
2. A design map for every scored experiment
3. A binary criterion-by-experiment table
4. Separate totals for experimental design, statistical methods, results, and validity
5. A separate table for any additional supplied checklist
6. A concise explanation of every 0, identifying the exact missing information
7. A direct comparison of the papers’ statistical transparency
8. A consistency audit confirming that tables and table notes received the same evidentiary status as prose

Do not produce a combined article-level score that averages primary, supporting, and diagnostic experiments.

## HTML presentation and scoring evidence

Save the output as a polished, self-contained HTML file that opens offline.

The HTML must include:

- Embedded styling
- Clear navigation
- Desktop, mobile, and print-friendly layouts
- Full paper titles
- Experiment classifications
- Source-scope statement
- A prominent notice that AI-generated judgments await human review
- Experiment-level category subtotals
- A direct comparison table
- A consistency audit
- Downloadable scored JSON embedded in the HTML

For every core criterion and supplied checklist item, show:

- Identifier
- Complete wording
- Overall score
- Concise rationale
- Required components
- Component-level scores
- Supporting evidence or exact absence explanation

For each evidentiary record, provide:

- A concise verbatim anchor, subject to applicable quotation limits
- A page-specific paraphrase explaining the relevant evidence
- One-based PDF page number
- Printed page number, where available
- Table, figure, section, note, or footnote locator

Make every score in the criterion matrix link to its evidence details.

For missing information:

- State exactly what is absent
- Identify the main-article pages and displays reviewed
- Do not invent a quotation to prove an absence
- Do not treat an appendix pointer as evidence that the missing information was reported

For N/A judgments:

- Explain why the concept cannot logically apply
- Exclude the item from the denominator

Show numerators, applicable-item denominators, and N/A counts for every subtotal.

Keep core scores, supplied-checklist scores, article-level items, and experiment-level items clearly separated. Do not double-count component scores in item totals.

The downloadable JSON and visible HTML tables must be generated from the same scored records.

## Final constraints

Do not give credit for customary practice, inferred implementation, or information available only outside the main article.

Do give full credit when the main-text table and its notes together make an analysis completely reconstructable.

Apply the same standard to every paper and report the resulting differences without seeking a predetermined winner.

Assessment scope

Include primary and embedded list experiments. Apply the list-experiment instructions and experiment classification rules in the shared research prompt.

Each paper is assessed in a separate GPT-5.6 Sol session at medium reasoning effort, using the shared prompt and main article.
